CHAPTER IX
Penalty Provisions
Article 70. Any person who comes under any one of the following
items shall be liable to penal servitude not
exceeding three (3) years or to fine not exceeding
300,000 yen, or to both, provided that in cases
where three (3) times the value of the goods con-
cerned exceeds 300,000 yen, the fine shal1 not
exceed three times the value of such goods.
(1) Any person who performed spot transactions of for-
eign exchange in violation of the provisions of
Article 7, Paragraph 5, in case the selling or buy-
ing rate for spot transactions is not fixed.
(2) Any person who violated the provisions of Article 7,
Paragraph 6.
(3) Any person who violated the provisions of Article. 8.
(4) Any person who engaged in the foreign exchange
business without obtaining the authorization under
Article 10, Paragraph 1.
(5) Any person who violated the suspension or restri-
ction under Article 13 (including the case of
application under Article 14, Paragraph 2).
(6) Any person (excluding foreign exchange banks) who
engaged in the money changers? business without
obtaining the authorization under Article 14,
Paragraph 1.
(7) Any person who violated the provisions of Article 26,
Paragraphs 1 and 2.

crgan of the Prime Minister's Office.

ECONOLIC AND SCIENTIFIC SECTION
Foreign Trade and Commerce Division
1 December 1949
EXPORTING FROM JAPAN
GENERAL
1. The Ministry of International Trade and Industry is the trade
control authority within the Japanese Government and the Foreign Exchange
Control oard is the exchange control authority.
2. Under Ministry of Finance Notification effective 5 December 1949,
a unitary r: te of exchange has been esteblished at6o to 1 and a cross-
rate of ¥lO08 to £1. Under this rate an FECB regulation has fixed bank
buying and selling rates at ¥358.45 and ¥361.55 respectiyely per U. S.
dollar; and ¥1003.66 and ¥1012.34 respectively per British pound.
: 3. .ll goods moved out of Japan are subject to appropriate clearance by
Customs, and cle rance by a Foreign Exchange Bank if the value of the export
exceeds 5000. In certain special cases a license of the Ministry of Inter-
national Trade and Industry is also required.
RETENTION OF FOHEIGN EXCHANGE
4. xporters in Japan are authorized to purchase foreign exchange for
yen on the basis of from 3 to 10 percent accruing from their private trade
export transections, the percentage allowed depending on the type of commo-
dity exported, The foreign exchange credits thus purchased may be used for
various pproved purposes such as travel abroad, payment of foreign agents!
commi sions, advertising and sales promotion, purchase information, catalogs,
patent rights, and samplcs, and for the importation of rw materials or
machinery designed to further cxport production.
5. In order to obtain forcign exchane, the exporter must submit to the
Linistry of International Trade and Industry an "Application for License to
Use and Purchase of uthorized Foreign xchange" (Form A). This license is
approved by the Ministry of Intornation:1 Trado and Industry and the Foreign
xchange Control oard, and is subj:ct to review by the Finance Ministry.
scAP validation is required only when the application is for travel abroad,
or for any case involving the use of exchange amounting to more than $10,000.
Detailed procudures are contained in Private Trade Information Memo No. 16.
APPOINTLENT OF OVERSEAS AGENTS.
6. xporters in Japan may appoint overseas agents as long as the agency
agreements do not violate the provisions of the Japanese Anti-Monopoly Law
(Law "54 of 1947), as amended. Commissions may bc paid to overseas agents
in foreign exchange under the procedure outlined in pare raph 6 above.
However, the amount of such commissions is subject to approval by the Japanese.
Government.
2

=== Page 527 === (confidence 0.99)
Exporting from Japan - Continued
PROCEDURES FOR PRIVATE EXPORTING
I. Frinciples
7. Buyers and sellers.may negotiate freely between themselves for sale
of exports and make private arrangements as to price, delivery, etc.
8. All export transactions may be handled directly through foreign
exchange benks (hereinafter, banks) which are authorized to certify Customs
Declaretion Forms (CDF) to clear shipment through Customs in accordance
with the law. Bank clearance is not required for exports valued at less
than ¥5000. The exporter, in such cases, is under a direct obligation to
effect a collction and surrender of the appropriate amount of foreign
exchange through the remittance procedure. (See paragrephs 43 and 44.)
9. Export licenses (to be issued by inistry of International Trade
and Industry - LIT ) are. required only:
a. For export of goods on a specific list to be found in Cabinet
Order dated 1 Jecember 1949 controlling export trade (attached hereto as
Annex 1);
b. In case a processing, barter, or other deal is involved which
would compensate for the export by related imports, or
c. In case paymonts are involved which are other than those
defined as "standerd payment terms" by the Foreign Exchange Control Board
(F¿CB) in its regulations governing payment terms dated 1 December 1949.
II. Operations
10. The exporter completes his deal and arranges for paymènt on a
private basis.
11. The exporter takes five (5) copies of his CDF to a bank for
indorsement together with a copy oi his approved license if required, and
evidence of payment.
12. If papers are in order and paymcnt arrangements are.satisfactory,
the bank certifies all five (5) copies of the CDF, sends one directly to
ITI and returns four (4) to the exporter.
13. The exporter takes four (4) copies of his CDF and three (3) copies
of his invoice to Customs, which checks that he has a bank indorsoment and
that his goods are as described thereon, and then certifies the CDFs and
invoices. Customs retains one (1) copy each of the CDF and invoice, and
returns one (1) copy of the cDF and two (2) copies of the invoice to the
exporter.
14. The exporter loads his goods and obtains complcted shipping
• documents evidencing shipment.
3

=== Page 528 === (confidence 0.99)
Exporting from Japan - Continued
15. The exporter returns to the bank with one (1) copy of the CDF,
two (2) copies of the invoices and his shipping documents. He negotiates
with the bank
a. for the sele of his documents directly for yen, or
b. for the bank to accept the documents for collection with yen
payment to be made when notice is received by the bank that collection has
been accomplished.
16. The banks forward the shipping documents abroad to the foreign
bank holding the foreign exchange credit posted by the buyer, direct that
deposit be mace to the SCAP ccount, and notify the FECB when the
transaction is conplete.
17. The above operation is the same in cases where an export license
is required cxccpt that in such cases, the bank will not indorse the CDF
until the license has bcen granted by ITI, and then will handle the
transcction for pymont as mey bo required by the license (if other than
standard payment terms are involved).
18. In case a license is required the exporter must apply directly to
IITI, filing four (4) license applications or heve the bank act on his behalf
with that ?gency.
19. 'An exporter exporting goods with the certification of a bank is
required to arrange colliction of payment in foreign exchange for the full
value of the goods through a foreign exchange bank promptly after the
export, in accordance with the certified paymcnt terms. hn exporter must
collect payment in forcign exchange within three (3) months from the day
following the day either or both of the following cases come to the attention
of the exporter.
a. hen payment terms were certified and negotiation or collection
of the forign exchange bill by e foreign exchango bank have been arranged,
but final payment is not honored by the drawee.
b. If the price of xport goots wes increased after shipment.
In the event the exporter cannot collect payment within the three (3)
months perioc, he may apply to MITI for a time extension.
III. Miscellaneous
20. Eleven (11) foreign exchange banks and their branches are
authorized initially to handle foreign exchange transactions.
21. SCAP licensed banks may service exporters in accordance with the
law.
22. MITI will operate fourteen (14) offices throughout Japan to
service exporters.
4

=== Page 529 === (confidence 0.99)
Exporting from Japan - Continued
23. Export transactions approved by the Japanese Government prior to
1 December may be processed until 15 December under the CDF in effect on-
30 November in order to preclude shipment delays which might result from
administrative causes.
SP CIAL CONDITIONS
I. Processing Deals
24. A processing doal by consignment requires a processing contract
between the foreign trader or consigner, who supplies all or a portion of
the raw rat rials to be processed into finished export goods and who takes
delivery th reof, and the supplier in Japan or consignee who processes'the
raw materials into finished export goods for delivery to the consignee
25. The supplying of sub-materials or packing materials only by a
consigner will not be considered a basis for a processing deal.
26. SCAP Form IE 40l (Revised 25 May 1949), Application for License to
Import, will be submittd to the Ministry of International Trade and Industry
(5 copies) to cover the import of rew materials to be processed, and the same
form will be submitted simultaneously to cover the raw'materials to be
imported es the processing fec.
27: Form I3 234, Application for License to xport (4 copies) will be
prepared concurrently to cover export of the processed goods.
28. There is nò specified form of contract covering processing by
consignment, however, any contract (3 copies) drawn between consigner and
consignee must incluce certain provisions specified by the Ministry of
International Trade and Industry. The approval of the contract by the
Ministry of International Trade and Industry must be sccured.
29. All risk or title in connection with raw materials forwarded for
processing will remain with tho consigner.
II. Compensatory Deals
30. If the export is to be compensated by a related import, the
exporter must file four (4) copies of I3 234 and two (2) copies of the contract
(or contracts) with Ministry of International Trade and Industry for approval.
31. When the exporter presents his CDF to the bank, it must be
accompanied by a copy of the license approved by the Ministry of International
Trade and Industry.
INVISIBLE EXPORTS
I. Domestic Sales
32. Domestic sales not over .500 or £25 may be made by use of form
JX 17 contract. Neither approval by the Ministry of International Trade
and Industry nor validation by the Supreme Commander for the Allied Powers
5

=== Page 530 === (confidence 0.99)
Exporting from Japan - Continued
is required for these transections. However, the use of form JX 17 is
rostricted to purchases of Japanese goods which are not to be shipped out
Japan or resold except by specially licensed commercial entrants in Japan.
Sale bove 500 or £25 in value require approval of the Ministry of Inter
national Trade and Industry only.
33. The buyer and seller negotiates a form JX 17 contract which is
signed by both partics. One (1) copy of the form JX 17 is presented to a
foreign exchange bank together with acceptable foreign exchange instruments
payable to the SCAP Comnercial Account or the SCAP Textile Account.
34. The foreign exchange processing bank stamps its receipt of payment
on the forn JX 17 which is then used as a basis for payment of yen to the
seller.
35. Domestic sales over $500 or £25 will be made by use of the form
JX 17 contract, which in such cases must be submitted in eight (8) copies
to the Ministry of International Trade and Industry for approval. When
approved, three (3) copies will be returned to the buyer and one (1) copy
to the seller. When the goods are ready for delivery, the buyer or his
agent (who may be the scller) submits two (2) copies of the contracts;
one marked "For Buyer Only", and one marked "Foreign Exchange Processing
Bank", and payment is made as outlined above.
36. Domustic sales in foreign cxchange are exempt temporarily from
excise and conswaption taxes.
II. Construction and epairs
37. Construction, repairs or renovation projects may be accomplished
by use of form JX 18 con' ract under the same procedure as applied to the
JX 17 contract. Projects up to 1000 or £250 in value do not require the
approval of the Supreme Ccmander for the Allied Powers or Ministry of
International Trade and Industry. Projects over $l000 or £250 require the
approval of the Ministry of Intornationel Trade and Industry only.
III. Centrol Purchasing Office or the Army  xchange Service
38. Sales to the Central Furchesing Office and contracts for construc-
tion made with the Central Purchasing Office or the Army Exchange Service
may be made directly for yen. The bill of materials for all construction
projects must bo certified by an engineer of the United States Army as
necessary to complote the projoct.
IV. OSS
39. Sales to the Overseas Supply Store are mede against a purchase order
prepared by Oss. hen delivery of goods is made they are receipted for on
the suppliur's copy of the purchase order which the supplier then presents
to a foreign exchange processing bank for payment of yen.

V. Sales to xport Bazaar
40. Sales to the various export bazaars now operating in Japan are
made by use of direct yen contrects between the supplier and the bazaar.
The contracts must be approved by the Supreme Commander for the Allied
Powers. All sales by the bazaar are made in foreign exchange, e repre-
sentative of à bank in each bazaar collecting the foreign exchenge for the
SCAP Commercial Account and disbursing yen peyment to the bezaar for payment
to the supplier and for operating expenses.
VI. U. S. Army Technical Services
41. Sales to the U. S. Army Tochnical Services are made under the
following procodure:
a. A written offer is submitted by the supplier to the technicel
service involved. This offer may be submitted in any form, provided it
contains the iniormation required by the technical services. Form IE 212
(without Ministry approval) may be usod for this purpose.
b. A "War Department Purchase Order" (Form D 18) is prepared by
the technical service whon the offer is accepted, and when availability of
funds is deterined, a copy of the offer and purchase order are forwarded to
the'supplier who then delivers the goods.
c. lhen paymont in dollars has been made to a SCAp Commercial Account
by the Army, an advice of collection is issued by the Supreme Commander for
the Allied Powers (Economic and Scientific Suction).
d. Upon receipt of the advice of collection, the supplier may apply
to a bank for payment of yen.
VII. Port Facilities
42. Port facilities, survices and supplius furnished to foreign vessels
for the account of the vessel are classified as invisible exports and are peid
for in acceptable foreign exchange uncier the following procedure:
a. Prices will be in accordance with Japanese Government laws and
regulations, or when not controllcd by law or regulation, will be the pre-
vailing price or as agrued upon by the supplier and use Prices in foreign
exchange will be obtained by applying the official exchange rate to the yen
price.
b. Suppliers must prepere bills and forward them together with sub-
stantiating documents to a consolidating agent (the local Jepanese Maritime
Bureau) as follows:
(1) Two (2) copies extended in U. S. dollars or Sterling only,
which must be confirmed by the shipping agency or other authorized agent as
to correctness of servicos performed end amount of charges.

(2) Three (3) copies extended in U.S. dollars or Sterlin
(3) Four (4) copies extended in yen only.
c. The bills for each vessel are consolidated by the consolidat
agent and processed through an authorized Japanese processing bank which
pays the supplier in yen.
d. The shipping agency, upon reccipt from consolidating agent of
three (3) copies of the consolidated invoice and copies of substantiating
bills and documents, forvards remittance in foreign exchange payable to the
order of SCAP Commercial Account to General Headquarters, Supreme Commander
for the Allied Powcrs, conomic and Scientific Section, Foreign Exchange
Funds Division. The flag of the vessel determines whether remittances must
be in U. S. dollars or Sterling. All remittances must be made in U. S.
dollars unless the vessel is under the flag of a Sterling hrea country.
VIII. Remittances to Cover Intangibles
43. Remittances are appropriate in payment for goods which are not
subject to export controls as indicated above (such as subscriptions to
publications) and to the sale of intangibles. For example, the sale of
Japanese rights in patents, tradumarks and copyrights held abroad, to the
extant such seles cre purmitted by Japanese law and instructions from the
Supreme Commander for the Allied Powers, should be paid for by remittance.
No approval by the inistry of International Trade and Industry or the Supreme
Commander for the Allied Powers is required. Similarly, the sale by a person.
in Japan of a right to apply abroad for a patont, copyright, or trademark
should be paid for by remittances. Gifts, peyments of obligations in Japen,
payments under approved foreign investments, ctc., should be made by remittance
If the romittance is associcted with en incidental tangible xport, appropriate
controls apply to the export. For example, if in selling the rights to
publish a manuscript, a copy of the menuscript is sent abroad by International
mail as e.snall packet, the appropriate docl ration form must be filed with
the postel cuthoritics.
44. When it is desired to remit funds to an individual or company in
Japan, it can be arranged through a bank transfer or check payable to the
SCAP Commercial Account or in such other manner as may be roguired by the
Foreign Exchange Control Board. The Japanese boneficiary is paid in yen
in Japan through the Japancso banking system.

III. Financial Control Provisions Enforced
In order to provide for srforcement of the financial control

Export Procedures after 1 December 1949
1. Generals
Private export trade is in principle free and all exports other thar
those of special commodities, those under processing deal or compensation
deal or those .under special payment terms which are not promulgateo by a
Foreign Exchange Control card Regulation do not require any approval by
license of the government agencies.
The certification of the bank is aiways required for export excepting
the case where a commodity of under ¥5,c00 in value or bona fide sample of
under ¥180,000 in estimate value is exported and it is the same as heretofore.
in that the custom will authorize the shipment only when such certification
is given by the bank.
The export procedures heretcfore in enfcrcement in which the application
for export license was required in principle is abolished and the export de-
.claretion form is used for ucumentation on vhich the certification of bank
is given.
However, in case of special export which requires the anproval by lis

=== Page 583 === (confidence 0.98)
of MiT1, the filing of aprlication for export license is required as same
as before and the certification of bank for such export is given provided
such epproved export license is presented along with the export declaration
form.
Although the government control on export is greatlyrelaxed and the
terms of contract is left to the discretion of contracting parties, it is
still strictly prchibited for the ex crter to reduce the price improperly by
dumping sales, flight of capitel or rebetes etc., and the person who committed
or intended to ccmtit sueh reduction will be subject to punishment by law.
MiTI must revier al tinds of expert ofter its shipment whether any
improner deal cr any viclation of laus or reguiations is involved in the
export.
Unclassified

=== Page 584 === (confidence 0.98)
- 2 -
2. Export License
a. Export requiring Approval by License of MITI. Following exports re-
quires the approval by license of MITI before the delivery.
The requirement for such license is defined in three ways, namely
commodities, contracts and payment terms.
(1) Export of commodities designated by the Cabinet Crder Attachment
which contains critical materials end others, such as food-stuffs,
metal products etc.
(2) Export under processing deal contrect or compensation deal, i.e.
under payment terms by escrow or back to back L/C.

3. Suspension of Loading.
In order to maintain sound international trade relations or.to avoid
any detrimental effect to the rehabilitation and development of national
economy, MITI may, in case of cratc emergency, suspend the loading of export
goods by designating the name of commodities or destinations by MITI Ordinance
within a limitation of a period less than one month.
The authority of ITI for suspension of loading stated above will be
applicable to all kinds of export before shipment, including the export for
which the approvel by license is given by MITI.
In case such suspension is enforced, MITI will give notice to the
foreign exchange banks (hereafter called as "bank") and the customs.

Cabinet Order concerning Ministerial Council
In accordance with the provision of ArticYe 3, paragraph 2 of the
Forcign Exchange and Foreign Trade Control Law (Law No:228 of 1949) the
Cabinet hereby establishes this Cabinet Order.
(Functions)
Article 1 The Ministerial Council (hereinafter referred to as the
Council) shall formulate and/or revise the foreign exchange budget
in accordance with the Foreign Exchange and Foreign Trade Control Law.
2. The Council shall take charge of such affairs in addition to that
(stipulated n the preceding paragraph, as may be placed under its jurimi
ction by Cabinet Order to be issued on the basis of the Foreign Exchang
and Foreign Trade. Control Law.

(Organization)

3. The members of the Council shall be as follows;
Minister of Finance
Finister of Agriculture and Forestry
Minister of International Trade and Industry
Minister of Transportation
Director General of the Economic Stabilization Board
(Chairman)
Article 3 The Chairman shall preside over the affairs of the Council.
(advisory Member).
Article 4 An advisory member shall be attached to the Council which
.position being filled by the Chairman of the Foreign Exchange Control Board.
2. The advisory member shall attend the meeting of thé Council and advise
the Council, on any matter placed under its juri sdiction..

Article 5. The Foreign Trade Bureau of the Economic Stabilization Boen

(Formulation of the Foreign Exchange Budget)
Article 6.
The Foreign Trade Bureau of the Economic Stabilization Board shall pre-

of this article and submit. it to the Council.
2. Not later then two (2) months prior to the commencement of the qu-
arter concerned shall be submitted to the Foreign Trade Bureau of the
Economic Stabilization Board the quarterly estimates of receipts and
